## 5.4 Done vs open, component by component

**(a) Recipes from own runs + human demos, deterministic replay, LLM fallback — partially done, and narrower after v2.** Done: executable skills induced from own trajectories (ASI, SkillWeaver, SpeedRunner, SkillDroid, PANDO); deterministic blueprints with LLM healing (Agentic Compilation, Hyperbrowser, LOOP); site-profile fast paths with verifier and fallback (Skim); hidden mined macros committed at runtime (SMC); **guarded GUI-trajectory replay with preconditions, bounded parameters, visual re-aiming and a promote/repair/quarantine lifecycle on OSWorld-Verified (EchoPath, Sep 2026)** [C15]; API-first execution that removes the GUI steps altogether (AXIS, ComputerRL, UFO2). Still open: no work combines own-run mining **with** a few human demonstrations and measures each source's marginal value; none calibrates the fallback trigger against a field-level error budget; none evaluates on multi-page, write-heavy enterprise forms with policy constraints; **no work measures replay success across UI versions over time** (EchoPath is a paired repeat-execution setting on 159 first-pass-validated tasks, against a Synapse memory baseline, with only a screen-resolution shift between passes; its offline start-state gate rejects 78% of incompatible starts) [C15; verified 2026-09-27]; de-optimization semantics (what state the LLM resumes from when a guard fails mid-recipe) are underexplored — Skim's warm start at the fast path's final URL and EchoPath's repair state are the closest [C, G, C15]. Also note ASI's finding that text-only workflow memory does not reliably cut steps (AWM 5.9 vs vanilla 5.6): the "text recipes" ablation is expected to show little efficiency gain, which strengthens the case for executable recipes.

**(b) Speculative execution of predictable UI transitions — done at the tool level; open for stateful live web UIs.** Done: Speculative Actions, PASTE, SMC, Speculate with Memory, AOSpec, DSP, ISP; snapshot/fork substrates (TClone, DeltaBox, SMC). Open: speculation in a live, proprietary, non-forkable SaaS app where server state cannot be snapshotted, which requires (i) restricting speculation to reversible/idempotent transitions (navigation, opening modals, pre-rendering, text entry), (ii) speculating the *observation* (predict the next page so the next model call starts early), or (iii) explicit compensating actions; no paper classifies web UI transitions by reversibility and reports hit rate and accuracy impact in such a portal. This is the most defensible systems contribution and the most likely to be scooped [C, G]. Two v2 additions sharpen it: Speculative Actions proves a **50% latency-reduction cap for single-step speculation**, so the p≈1.0 transitions in Concur should push single-step hit rates toward that cap and *multi-step* (macro-commit-style) speculation is required to exceed it; and every speculative system in doc 15 (SPORK, Speculative Actions, PASTE, DSP) falls back to serial execution for writes — "no paper reports speculative or multi-action execution on a live OSWorld desktop with controlled accuracy measurement" [C15] (with the caveat in D46 that UFO2 and OSWorld 2.0 do report multi-action *batching* results).

**(c) Observation pruning, prefix caching, small-model routing — done.** AgentOccam, FocusAgent, MFS, Agentic Compilation's sanitizer; Skim's small-model extraction; RouteLLM/BoPO/WebRouter; provider prompt caching. Novelty ≈ zero; the only publishable angle is the **interaction**: pruning invalidates caches (AgentDiet notes it), routing interacts with recipe coverage — quantify the interactions rather than the levers [C, G].

**(d) Accuracy–latency–cost frontiers over repeated runs — partially done.** AI Agents That Matter and HAL do 2-D accuracy–$ frontiers; τ-bench introduced pass^k; SpeedRunner reports ±1 s.d. over 3 seeds. No work reports **three-way** accuracy–latency–cost frontiers with **field-level** accuracy for form-filling agents over repeated runs, or attributes accuracy loss to each lever [C].

**The combination and the setting.** The integrated stack on an enterprise portal is not published as a unit; no LLM-agent paper evaluates on SAP Concur or expense reporting with policy-derived keys (closest: TheAgentCompany finance/admin tasks; WorkArena/++ on ServiceNow). Reviewers value the answer-key methodology more than the brand [C].

## 5.5 Baselines, ablations, objections (merged)

**Baselines reviewers will demand.** (1) Vanilla ReAct / Browser-Use / BrowserGym GenericAgent on the same model (the baseline of Agent JIT, Skim, FocusAgent). (2) AWM (text workflows) and ASI or SkillWeaver (programmatic skills as tools); SpeedRunner optional. (3) Pure RPA/scripted replay from demos with no LLM, plus the same with naive LLM fallback (Hyperbrowser / Agentic-Compilation style) — the most dangerous baseline: if a script alone reaches 95% field accuracy at near-zero cost, the contribution is only the residual. (4) Skim-style fast path with verifier and warm-started fallback. (5) Speculative Actions and SMC-style macro commit on equal hardware (SMC reports gains relative to SA; do the same). (6) APC plan caching. (7) Prompt-caching-only, small-model-only, cascade/router-only (FrugalGPT/RouteLLM), each alone. (8) Agent JIT Compilation if code is available, else a re-implementation of its planner. (9) Inference-optimized stateless baseline on SGLang/vLLM APC to isolate infrastructure gains. (10) PANDO and SkillDroid adaptations [C, G]. Added in v2 [C15]: (11) **an API-first / hybrid API+GUI agent** (AXIS-, ComputerRL- or UFO2-style) wherever Concur exposes an API — if it solves most reports, the GUI stack is only for the API-less remainder; (12) **EchoPath-style guarded trajectory replay** with lifecycle management, the closest published recipe system; (13) a **budget-shown ReAct / BATS-style** baseline, since showing the agent its remaining budget alone cut cost 31% at equal accuracy.

**Ablations.** Leave-one-out for (a), (b), pruning, caching, routing; pairwise interactions (caching × pruning; recipes × routing); recipe source (own runs only / demos only / both; 0, 1, 3, 5 demos; number of own runs); fallback-trigger threshold sweep (produces the frontier itself); speculation depth/breadth and reversible-only vs all transitions with hit rate, wasted calls and side-effect incidents; pruning aggressiveness vs field accuracy; N ≥ 5 repeated runs with pass^k and bootstrap CIs on every frontier point; distribution shift (synthetic DOM perturbations, a real Concur release, new policy rules and expense types) with recipe breakage and recovery cost; SpeedRunner-style cost-per-report curves vs number of prior reports including amortized compilation; w/o speculative UI execution (quantifies the 71% model-wait that faster LLMs alone cannot remove) [C, G].

**Objections and pre-emptions.**

| Objection | Pre-emption |
|---|---|
| Single-domain testbed / SAP Concur overfitting | Add WorkArena/WorkArena++, WebArena (or WebArena-Verified Hard subset), τ²-bench (AppWorld as alternative). Measure UI-transition entropy in Concur vs open-web benchmarks; show graceful degradation to exploratory execution when entropy is high. Show the 57% page-determined, p≈1.0 and 71% model-wait statistics generalize (Skim's 66.7% navigational steps is an external anchor) |
| Live proprietary portal is not reproducible | Release a Concur-like simulator or offline record-and-replay artifact (Mind2Web-style), anonymized policies and keys; put headline claims on public benchmarks and position Concur as the case study; report portal version and dates |
| It's engineering, not research | Contribute (i) a formal model of expected speedup vs recipe coverage, guard precision and speculation hit rate (Speculative Actions' cost–latency analysis is a template); (ii) safety semantics for speculation without snapshots (reversibility classification; commit/compensation); (iii) a measurement methodology (field-level, 3-D frontiers) |
| Fragility of speculation; non-Markovian rollbacks | Rigid safe/unsafe classification: navigation, pagination, text entry may be speculated; POST/approvals/final submissions never; quantify rollback overhead and side-effect incidents |
| Answer-key leakage into recipe learning | Learn recipes only from trajectories and demos; keys only for evaluation; disjoint report sets; report results when recipe learning uses only self-judged success |
| Comparison fairness (caching, hardware, provider latency) | Report $ with and without caching (HAL reports without); fix region, time of day, model version; report API latency distributions; give speculative baselines equal compute |
| Lossy acceleration hides errors | Report per-field error deltas and worst-case field; include an abstain/escalate-to-human option in the frontier |
| **"Why click through Concur at all? Use the API."** (raised by AXIS, ComputerRL, UFO2, Beyond Browsing — added in v2 [C15]) | Measure and report the workflow's API coverage (which fields/steps have a usable, permitted API; which policy checks live only in the UI); include an API-first baseline where an API exists; position GUI recipes + speculation as the method for the API-less, permission-gated remainder that most enterprise portals still have; cite AXIS's like-for-like agent comparison (API-first vs UI agent on Word: ≈2× faster, 3.2→2.0 steps, cost halved, 52→84% success) as what API access buys; its −65–70% is against manual human operation |
| **"Guarded replay with lifecycle already exists" (EchoPath, Sep 2026)** | Compare directly on OSWorld-Verified repeat tasks; differentiate on demo mixing, speculation, field-level error attribution, write-heavy multi-page forms, and replay success measured across UI versions over time — none of which EchoPath reports; note EchoPath's baseline is another memory system (Synapse, 91.8% vs EchoPath-Codex 91.2%), not a no-memory agent; its gain is cost/time at equal success; its code link was 404 on 2026-09-27 |

## 5.6 Framings

1. **Profile-guided tiered compilation for agents (JIT with de-optimization).** Interpret with the LLM; detect hot, stable trajectory fragments; compile them into guarded recipes; de-optimize to the LLM on guard failure, resuming from the current state. Venue: MLSys (OSDI/SOSP if the runtime is substantial). Position against Agent JIT (compiles from the instruction, not profiles), Agentic Compilation (no guards/profiling), SpeedRunner/ASI/SkillDroid (skills as LLM-called tools or mobile-only), APC. Distinguishing claim: profile-guided + guard-based de-optimization with measured coverage and mis-speculation cost [C, G].
2. **Branch prediction for stateful UIs: speculative execution without snapshots.** Treat UI transitions as branches; predict and execute (or pre-fetch the observation of) near-deterministic transitions; classify by reversibility with commit and compensation rules. Venue: MLSys or NeurIPS. Position against Speculative Actions (general, lossless), SMC/TClone (need snapshots), PASTE (tools), Speculate with Memory (transition tables), Skim (read-only). Distinguishing claim: safe speculation in live, irreversible web apps with hit-rate and side-effect analysis [C, G].
3. **Where agent time and money go: field-level, three-way Pareto accounting for enterprise automation.** A measurement paper on answer-key-graded workflows decomposing the 13-minute, 100-call run into page-determined vs reasoning steps and reporting per-lever accuracy loss with repeated runs and consistency metrics. Venue: NeurIPS D&B or ICLR; strongest with a released Concur-like environment. Position against AI Agents That Matter, HAL, Efficient Agents (2-D), τ-bench pass^k, WorkArena++ [C]. Gemini's "microeconomics of agentic automation" is the same framing [G].

Recommendation (both providers): lead with 1 + 2 as one MLSys submission and use 3's methodology as the evaluation backbone; 3 alone risks reading as a case study unless an environment is released.

## 5.7 Public benchmarks and cost of a full ablation grid (reconciled)

| Benchmark | Why it fits | Size / steps | Cost per run (reported → extrapolated) | Grid (~9 configs × 5 repeats = 45 runs) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **WorkArena L1 + WorkArena++ L2/L3** (ServiceNow; NeurIPS 2024 D&B) | Enterprise forms/lists with human-coded oracles; closest public analogue to Concur; free developer instances; human success 93.9%; GPT-4o 2.1–43% depending on level | ++ has 682 tasks (235 each L2/L3 in the standard curriculum), 50-step limits, up to ~140 oracle actions; L1 eval = 33 tasks × 10 seeds = 330 episodes | **Measured:** L1 $55.6 per 330-episode run (GPT-4.1 GenericAgent, 53.6%); BrowserGym Claude 3.5 Sonnet: L1 $0.303/task, L2 $1.274/task, L3 $0.815/task. Extrapolated: L2+L3 ≈$250–300 per run at GPT-4.1-class prices | L1 ≈$2.5k; L2+L3 ≈$11–14k; 5–10× less with small models. **Gemini's "$15–50 per full run" is inconsistent with the measured $55.6 for L1 alone — do not use** |
| **WebArena** (812 tasks; or WebArena-Verified Hard 258) | Where AWM, ASI, SkillWeaver, AgentOccam, FocusAgent report; self-hosted Docker | 30-step limit (BrowserGym) | **Measured:** $59.0 on a 381-task subset (GPT-4.1, 36.5%); BrowserGym Claude 3.5 Sonnet $0.171/task. Extrapolated ≈$125 per full run | ≈$2.7k (381 subset) / ≈$5.6k (full); Hard-258 subset cuts ≈83% of tasks while keeping the difficult ones [G]. Gemini's "$50–100 per full run" is plausible only for cheap models |
| **τ²-bench** (50 airline / 115 retail / 114 telecom) | Policy-constrained multi-turn workflows mirroring policy-derived keys; native pass^k; SMC and HAL comparators | Dual-control | **Measured:** ~$40 per trial, all domains, gpt-4.1; HAL τ-Airline $0.31 (Gemini 2.0 Flash, 28%) to $180.49 (Opus 4.1, 54%); o4-mini High $11.36 at 56% | ≈$1.8k at gpt-4.1 prices; $7k+ with 4 trials/task |
| Alternatives: **AppWorld** (750 tasks, 457 APIs; GPT-4o ~49% normal / ~30% challenge; state-based collateral-damage tests; SMC comparator; ~$0.02–0.03/task with a small model, unverified); **TheAgentCompany** (175 tasks; ~$735 per run at $4.20/task — subset only); **OSWorld/OSWorld-Verified/OSWorld-Pro** (desktop; supplementary if visual SoM matters; ~$10–20 VM per run before vision-model API costs) | | | | [C, G] |

## 5.8 Recommendations (merged)

1. Re-run the novelty search every 2–4 weeks; speculative agent execution had ≥8 arXiv submissions in mid-2026; SMC (Sep 2026) is the most dangerous overlap for the speculation component and **EchoPath (Sep 2026) for the recipe component** [C, C15].
1b. Before building, audit Concur's API surface and state the API-coverage number in the paper; reviewers will ask (§5.5) [C15].
2. Make pure-RPA-replay and Skim/Agent-JIT baselines central; if scripts alone solve most reports, say so and claim the residual [C].
3. Scope speculation to what is safe without snapshots (observation pre-fetch; reversible navigation); report side-effect incidents as a metric [C, G].
4. Publish the characterization statistics (57% page-determined, p≈1.0 transitions, 71% model wait) on public benchmarks, and add the UI-transition-entropy measurement [C, G].
5. Report field-level accuracy loss per lever with CIs over N ≥ 5 runs and 3-D frontiers; include cached vs uncached $ [C].
6. Validate on WorkArena++ first for reproducibility; keep Concur as the real-world case study [G, C].

# PART VII — Provider scorecard (取长补短)

| Dimension | Claude [C] | ChatGPT [O] | Gemini [G] |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Source hygiene** | Consistent primary/secondary labeling; flags paper-internal inconsistencies (OSWorld-Human $2.43; Efficient Agents; τ-bench); exact table extraction (OSWorld-Human Table 1) | Best: measurement-definition discipline (calls ≠ steps ≠ turns; inference ≠ wall-clock; attempt ≠ success); version-aware (uses revised tables: FocusAgent Aug 2026, Continuum v7, PASTE v3, Agentix NSDI); labels every derived number as "calculated"; explicitly says what was not retrieved | Weakest: cites trackers, blogs, a Web3 Medium post, a YouTube video, an awesome-list, a personal homepage; cites an AI-generated idea file as a paper (MacroPO); repeats uncorrected abstracts (Efficient Agents); mis-states APC and Speculate-with-Memory; misattributes OSWorld 2.0's finding; stale/inconsistent pricing; Google-Docs export lost formulas to images |
| **Unique, valuable finds** | HAL per-task cost table; OSWorld-Human observation-cost (3–26 s AXTree) and 4–12 calls/judge; Google 3.2Q tokens; Microsoft Foundry 128k downgrade; unified Sept-2026 price table; power calculation; the 2026 novelty landscape (Agent JIT, Skim, SMC, SpeedRunner, AAPT, LOOP, Activity Frames, Hyperbrowser); UI-CUBE; Efficient Benchmarking (Ndzomga); DeltaSelect. **P3 run (doc 15, v2):** ComputerRL, AXIS, Beyond Browsing, ECLAIR, EchoPath, AgentReuse, ToolCaching, KVCOMM, DroidSpeak, LLM-Tool Compiler, ParaGUI, ScreenSeekeR, Overthinking (Cuadron), BATS, TTI; Speculative Actions' 50% single-step cap; ASI's AWM-uses-more-steps re-run; vendor effort numbers (Opus 4.5 −76% tokens, GPT-5 −45% tool calls); warming $2.45 vs LATS $134.50; venue confirmations (WALT ICLR 2026 poster, AI Agents That Matter TMLR 2025, DSP ICLR 2026 proceedings, Prune4Web AAAI 2026); the OSWorld-Human MLSys camera-ready PDF; the clearest "lossless vs lossy" classification per family | BrowserGym per-task timings for WebArena/WorkArena; original OSWorld/WebArena human stopwatch times; Online-Mind2Web Operator 44 min / 2.6×; OpenRouter 2026 telemetry (15×, Feb crossover, 14×, 70% cached); Anthropic 4×/15×; OpenAI Codex 63.3%; Gartner primary; Temporal survey; "Why Johnny Can't Use Agents"; Agentic Test-Time Scaling (9.6×); Webwright; Claude in Chrome → Haiku default; Anthropic CU best practices; Agentic Coding in the Wild; AWO "fewer calls ≠ faster"; StepWise; Fara-7B; AQuaUI; TRACE; GoClick; PreAct overhead; binomial bound; correction of the Browser Use "task time" | Mind2Web 2 human timing (8.4 vs 18.4 min); HUMAN Security 7,851%; Cost of Dynamic Reasoning energy figures; EY $0.04→$1.20; Gartner Aug-2026 5× workflow-cost release; Gartner Apr-2026 ROI survey; Operator timeline; the broad 2026 systems sweep (TokenPilot, PEEK, SpecBox, DeltaBox, CacheScout, Helium, AsymCache, AgentKVShift, DualPath, Atomix, Spork, Cordon, AgentServe); SkillDroid, PANDO, MFS/GEPA, Prune4Web, SWE-Pruner, Qwen-UI-Agent, CUA-Universe; Odysseys, Reliability Science Framework (VAF), best@k/avg@k; "environment 53.7%" and "tools 35–61%" latency shares; business/TCO framing |
| **Errors / weaknesses** | Mislabeled Browser Use inference time as task time (D1); imprecise on OSWorld 2.0 human timing (D2); internal v1/v2 inconsistency across its own reports (D39); missed BrowserGym timings, the OpenRouter 2026 telemetry, the HCI study and the controlled TTS experiment. **P3 run (doc 15):** uses FocusAgent v1 and AppAgentX v1 numbers (D44, D48); repeats Efficient Agents' abstract figures (D9); claims no controlled multi-action study exists on OSWorld (D46); leaves Autellix's venue unverified although NSDI 2026 exists (D52); its Ares WebArena numbers are self-flagged automated extraction (D49); and it explicitly did not check OSWorld 2.0 or TheAgentCompany — the two benchmarks that carry Part I | Two survey runs disagree on FocusAgent (D15) and quote DSP against different baselines; terse to the point of density; no novelty run in the set; some Speculate-with-Memory nuance (1.05×) may be over-conservative relative to the paper's own estimates | Listed above; also benchmark cost estimates too low (WorkArena++ "$15–50"), "median" for a mean (D3), "88%" (D19), Gemini 2.5 Flash TTFT via secondary, EY/Uber/TCO figures unverified, off-topic robotics papers in the survey |
| **Best use of each** | Backbone for evidence tables, pricing, novelty positioning, and statistical protocol | Backbone for definitions, corrections, budget sensitivity, and the "what papers actually report" audit | Idea generator for breadth (2026 systems papers, business framing) — every item must be re-verified before citation |

Which document to open for what: evidence-with-caveats → doc 14 (O) then doc 8 (C); mapping table → doc 1 (O) and doc 7 (C); landscape tables → docs 2 and 3 (O) and doc 15 (C, best lossless/lossy classification and 2026 systems coverage) with doc 13 (G) as a lead list; protocol and budget → docs 4 (O) and 5 (C); novelty → doc 6 (C) with doc 11 (G) for SkillDroid/PANDO/MFS; business framing → doc 12 (G) with verification.

# PART VIII — Gaps, verification queue, and suggested next investigations

## 8.1 Evidence nobody found (true gaps)

- Side-by-side **agent and human wall-clock on identical tasks** (OSWorld-Human's human side is an estimate; OSWorld 2.0's is an annotator estimate; original OSWorld/WebArena stopwatch times use different samples).
- **First-party agentic token share** from OpenAI/Anthropic/Microsoft (only Codex output-token shares and Anthropic's 4×/15×); analyst token-share reports (Morgan Stanley, Goldman Sachs, SemiAnalysis, Epoch AI, Artificial Analysis).
- Per-task **timing/call/token datasets for Claude computer use, Claude in Chrome, Operator/ChatGPT agent, ChatGPT Work cloud browser**.
- **Quantified HCI wait-tolerance** for computer-use agents (only the qualitative Johnny study).
- Consulting-firm **latency-blocker percentages** (McKinsey, BCG, Bain, Deloitte, KPMG, PwC).
- Primary **Groq/Cerebras/SambaNova/Browserbase** agent-speed claims.
- A standard **accounting unit** (calls per solved task + prefill tokens + turns + tool time).
- **Replay success across UI versions over time** for any skill/recipe/cache system, and any study of **cache poisoning** in shared agent caches (added in v2 from doc 15).
- **Any serving-system evaluation on a GUI/computer-use workload** (all reviewed serving papers use ReAct/SWE/BFCL/multi-agent workloads).
- **A whole-stack end-to-end measurement** (lossless serving + call-count reducers + lossy levers) on a computer-use benchmark with dollars and wall-clock — the opening the Part V plan can take.

## 8.3 Suggested follow-up deep-research prompts (for the next round)

1. **Latency decomposition by architecture** — "For each of: screenshot-based CUAs (OSWorld/OSWorld-Verified agents), AXTree/DOM web agents (BrowserGym, Browser Use, Skim), and cached coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, Copilot), find every published per-step latency breakdown (model prefill/decode, screenshot/AXTree generation, browser/tool execution, queueing) with the harness, model, caching state and date; produce a table that states which term dominates and why."
2. **Speculation safety semantics for stateful web UIs** — "Survey all 2025–2026 work on speculative/asynchronous execution of side-effecting tool or UI actions (Atomix, Cordon, TClone, DeltaBox, SMC, AOSpec, Speculative Actions, PASTE, Spork): what isolation/rollback/compensation mechanisms each assumes, which are feasible on a live SaaS portal without snapshots, and what hit rates and side-effect incidents they report."
3. **Enterprise RPA + LLM fallback products** — "Document what UiPath, Automation Anywhere, Microsoft Power Automate, Hyperbrowser, Browserbase, Browser Use, LOOP and similar ship for record/replay with LLM fallback: failure detection, selector healing, measured replay success, cost per run, and any published latency numbers."
4. **Human-time baselines** — "Find every benchmark that reports timed human completion (OSWorld §3.4, WebArena, Mind2Web 2, OSWorld 2.0, WorkArena++, GDPval, METR time horizons, APEX-Agents, Agents' Last Exam) with the timing method, sample and task overlap with agent runs; identify where a clean agent-vs-human wall-clock ratio can be computed."
5. **2026 agent-serving papers** — "Verify and tabulate CacheScout, Helium, TokenPilot, PEEK, SpecBox, DeltaBox, AsymCache, AgentKVShift, DualPath, UNISON, PBKV, AgentServe, Continuum v7, Agentix: baseline, hardware, workload, headline gain, quality reporting, and whether code is released."
6. **Vendor speed tiers and computer-use latency** — "Collect primary documentation for every latency-oriented tier or product move since Oct 2025 (Anthropic fast mode and Claude in Chrome defaults; OpenAI Fast/Flex; Microsoft Foundry priority; Google Flash/Flash-Lite computer use; Groq, Cerebras, SambaNova agent claims; Browserbase/Browser Use) with prices, measured tokens/s, TTFT and any end-to-end computer-use timing."

# Appendix B — Glossary of measurement terms used in this dossier

- **Step / turn**: one observe→decide→act cycle against the environment (OSWorld 2.0 uses "steps" in Table 3 and "turns" in the text; batched tool calls put several actions in one step).
- **Tool call**: one action or tool invocation; several per step when batched.
- **Model call**: one LLM request; several per step in planner/judge/reflection scaffolds (GTA1: 4–12 planning calls per judging call).
- **Critical-path depth**: the longest chain of dependent model/tool calls; governs latency under parallel execution.
- **Wall-clock**: request accepted → final committed state, including environment time, tool time, queueing and rate-limit waits.
- **Inference time**: model-only decision time; excludes browser/tool/environment time (Browser Use's 68 s vs 225–330 s).
- **Prefill / decode**: input-processing vs token-generation phases; prefill-bound when uncached contexts grow (OSWorld-Human), decode-bound when caches hold (2605.26297).
- **Cost per attempt / per success / cost-of-pass**: mean $ per attempted task; total $ ÷ successes (pooled); taskwise E[C_i]/p_i.
- **pass^k / pass@k**: probability all k trials succeed / any of k succeeds.
- **WES**: Weighted Efficiency Score — success weighted by step efficiency vs human reference (OSWorld-Human).
- **Lossless (sense 1/2/3)**: by-construction trajectory preservation / statistical non-inferiority within δ / no observed degradation.
- **Regime A/B/C**: latency profiles for screenshot multi-call agents / AXTree single-call web agents / cached coding agents (§1.8).

*End of dossier.*
